Nvidia reinstates founder Jensen Huang after Trump-backed board reversal

Category: AI

Nvidia’s board reinstated co-founder Jensen Huang as president and chief executive on Nov. 23, reversing its decision to accept his resignation 11 days earlier. The board said it was satisfied with Huang’s candor and leadership after meeting with him, and that senior U.S. government officials had advised it that his return posed no national security risk [2][9][11]. The reversal followed President Donald Trump’s public calls for Huang to return. Trump said he had spoken with the board and “convinced them”; TechCrunch, citing Semafor, reported that Trump dialed into a board meeting. Nvidia’s statement referred to input from senior government officials [26][49]. The board’s independent inquiry into reports that Huang was targeted by a foreign intelligence effort remains open, as does separate scrutiny of potential diversion of restricted chips to China [79][31]. Nvidia shares rose 31.5% on Monday, adding $952 billion to its market value, while the Nasdaq 100 gained 6.9% [26]. Huang is scheduled to appear before three House committees on Dec. 1 and testify publicly before the Senate Banking Committee on Dec. 3 [31][26].

Primary Source: View original article

I take this responsibility with the greatest humility and renewed commitment.

— Jensen Huang, Nvidia co-founder and chief executive (WIRED)

Did you know: Nvidia’s chief executive has about 60 direct reports, a detail analysts cited as an example of the company’s dependence on its founder [302].

Key Points

  • Board oversight: Nvidia has formed a committee of independent directors to oversee national-security and export-control risks, and has begun searching for a chief compliance officer [101][171].
  • Political pressure: Trump urged Nvidia to continue its export-control review after the reinstatement [140][161].
  • Export controls: The U.S. added Singapore-based Megaspeed and six related firms to a trade blacklist over suspected diversion of Nvidia AI chips; Megaspeed denies wrongdoing [90][165].
  • Asian suppliers: Shares of Nvidia suppliers also rose in Tuesday trading. Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Co. gained nearly 10%, while South Korea’s Samsung Electronics and SK Hynix each rose about 6.7% [54].
  • Industry backing: Tech executives including Elon Musk, Replit CEO Amjad Masad and Epic Games CEO Tim Sweeney publicly urged Huang’s return [23].
  • Employee divide: Some Nvidia employees welcomed Huang with cheers, while others had hoped his departure would prompt changes to what workers described as a culture that discouraged challenging leadership [150].
  • Market reaction: Nvidia shares rose more than 30% on the reinstatement news, adding more than $1 trillion to its market value during Monday trading [90][13].
  • Export oversight: Nvidia has established a National Security and Export Control Committee [69][79].
  • Interim leadership: Chief Financial Officer Colette Kress, who served as interim CEO after Huang resigned, will remain Nvidia’s CFO and executive vice president [38].
  • Chip controls: Sen. Tom Cotton urged passage of the proposed Chip Security Act, which would require chipmakers to track advanced AI processors and notify the government if they suspect the chips are being sold to prohibited users [231][275].
  • Wider inquiries: Separate scrutiny includes a Justice Department probe into Megaspeed, a reported Securities and Exchange Commission inquiry into Nvidia’s accounting and shareholder disclosures, and an investigation by California’s attorney general [211][203].
  • Internal backing: WIRED reported that more than 1,600 employees urged Huang’s reinstatement in an internal suggestion-box post; TechCrunch said an open letter drew more than 200,000 signatures [9][28].
  • Workforce divide: More than 200,000 people, including about 10,000 current Nvidia employees, signed an open letter calling for Huang’s return [29].
  • Megaspeed restrictions: The U.S. Commerce Department placed Singapore-based Nvidia customer Megaspeed and six other firms on an export blacklist, requiring licenses to buy Nvidia AI chips. The department cited the risk of diversion to unauthorized users [371].
  • AI model competition: Huang has backed open-weight AI models that companies can run themselves, a strategy that could challenge subscription-based labs such as Anthropic. Anthropic shares fell during Monday’s broader AI rally [370][364].
  • Congressional pushback: Sen. Tom Cotton said Congress should pursue bipartisan legislation tracking advanced-chip exports, while Senate Democratic leader Chuck Schumer pledged to investigate possible wrongdoing by administration officials and Nvidia executives [128][199].
  • Compliance questions: Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent said the administration had not received satisfactory answers from Nvidia about whether any suspected violations reflected isolated conduct or a broader compliance failure [181][93].

Perspectives

Nvidia board: Chair Harvey C. Jones said the directors believed Huang was the right leader and that his return served shareholders, employees, partners and customers [2][9].

Sources: WIRED

President Donald Trump: Trump said Nvidia’s board was easier to persuade after the stock had fallen 40–50% and that his intervention was important [33].

Sources: PCMag

Sen. Elizabeth Warren: The Massachusetts Democrat called the reinstatement “reckless,” according to Euronews, citing concerns about the president’s role and unresolved export-control questions [62].

Sources: Euronews

Dan Niles, founder of Niles Investment Management: Niles said he was trimming his Nvidia position after the rebound, arguing that the risks present before Huang’s resignation remained [45].

President Donald Trump: Trump argued that losing Huang posed a greater threat to Nvidia than keeping him and said the board had asked the administration for assurances about national-security concerns [135][33].

Sources: Fast Company

Nvidia employees who opposed the return: Some staffers saw Huang’s departure as a chance to change workplace practices they said tolerated abusive behavior and discouraged employees from challenging leadership [150].

Sources: WIRED

Corporate governance experts: Governance experts told The New York Times that Trump’s push effectively overrode a board that had said Huang would not return [1].

Sources: The New York Times

U.S. lawmakers critical of the decision: Some lawmakers questioned Huang’s reinstatement while investigations remained ongoing [169].

Sources: Firstpost

Nvidia employees: Hundreds greeted Huang with cheers, applause and hugs at the company’s Santa Clara headquarters after his return [86].

Sources: Firstpost

President Donald Trump: He argued that Huang’s return was important to keeping the U.S. competitive in AI, warning that China could recruit the Nvidia chief and gain ground in the technology race [193].

Sources: The Hindu

U.S. lawmakers: Republican Sen. John Curtis questioned the administration’s role in Huang’s reinstatement and officials’ national security assurances while inquiries remain open [128].

Sources: Firstpost

China’s Foreign Ministry: Chinese officials rejected claims that Alice Huang worked for Chinese intelligence, describing the allegations as slander [243][70].

Sources: TASS

U.S. lawmakers: Sen. Elizabeth Warren called the reinstatement reckless and demanded an explanation of what government officials told Nvidia’s board [279].

Sources: Financial Times

Charles Elson, corporate governance expert: The University of Delaware professor said the board’s reversal weakened its credibility and could undermine enforcement of Nvidia’s code of conduct [78].

Sources: CBS News

Jeff Herbst, former Nvidia executive: Herbst said the episode could be an opportunity to strengthen the company’s governance, succession planning and management as Nvidia has grown into a much larger business [302].

Sources: CNBC

Dan Niles, founder of Niles Investment Management: Niles said he was reducing his Nvidia position after the rally, arguing that the company’s unresolved risks remained despite Huang’s return [295].

Sources: CNBC

Timeline

  • 29 October 2026: The New York Times reported an alleged Chinese spy plot targeting Huang [70].
  • 9 November 2026: Nvidia’s board opened a probe after Huang acknowledged an affair [70].
  • 12 November 2026: Huang resigned; CFO Colette Kress became interim CEO [9].
  • 17 November 2026: Huang said he would return if the board judged it best [9].
  • 23 November 2026: Nvidia’s board unanimously reinstated Huang after discussions and government input [2].
  • 1 December 2026: Huang is expected to speak with three House committees behind closed doors [31][49].
  • 3 December 2026: Huang is scheduled for public testimony before the Senate Banking Committee [26].
  • 29 October 2026: The New York Times reported Huang’s close relationship with Alice Huang [129]
  • 8 November 2026: Huang acknowledged the affair as Nvidia opened an independent investigation [129]
  • 12 November 2026: Huang resigned; finance chief Colette Kress became interim CEO [9]
  • 17 November 2026: Board chair Harvey Jones said Nvidia did not intend to reinstate Huang [87]
  • 23 November 2026: Nvidia’s board reversed course and restored Huang to leadership [23]
  • 1 December 2026: Huang is scheduled for closed-door interviews with three House committees [31]
  • 3 December 2026: Huang is expected to testify publicly before the Senate Banking Committee [174]

  • 1 December 2026: Huang is scheduled for closed-door House committee interviews [178][211]
  • 3 December 2026: Huang is due to testify at a public Senate hearing [178][211]
  • 12 November 2026: Huang resigned after reports of relationships and national-security concerns [5][284]
  • 23 November 2026: Nvidia’s board voted unanimously to restore Huang as CEO [5][13]
  • 1 December 2026: Huang is scheduled for closed-door interviews with three House committees [344]
  • 3 December 2026: Huang is scheduled to testify publicly before the Senate Banking Committee [279]

Industry Impact

  • Semiconductor supply chain: Asian chip suppliers also rallied after Huang’s reinstatement. Taiwan’s Taiex rose 9%, while South Korea’s Kospi gained about 7%; TSMC and Nvidia’s memory-chip partners Samsung and SK Hynix also advanced [51].
  • Asian semiconductor supply chain: Investors drove chip and technology shares higher across Asia; Samsung Electronics and SK Hynix rose as much as 7.6% and 8.4%, respectively, while TSMC gained almost 10% [223].
  • AI startup funding: Nvidia had invested nearly $50 billion in AI model makers by the end of July and pledged $170 billion in investment-grade credit to AI companies and cloud providers [310].
  • Chip supply chain: Nvidia accounts for close to a fifth of Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Co.’s revenue [221].
  • AI model competition: Nvidia has backed open-model developers and promoted downloadable Nemotron models, an approach that could pressure subscription-based providers such as Anthropic if customers adopt self-hosted alternatives [24][30].
  • AI-linked markets: Huang’s return lifted related U.S. equities, with cloud-computing provider CoreWeave up 22.32%, while the Nasdaq Composite advanced 6.51% on Monday [258][5].

Historical Background

Huang co-founded Nvidia in 1993 and led it for 33 years before resigning. Under his leadership, the company grew from a video-game chip designer into a leading supplier of AI processors [67][14]. In the days before the reinstatement, board chair Harvey Jones had publicly said Nvidia did not intend to bring Huang back [9][84].

Business Angle

  • Nvidia accounts for close to a fifth of TSMC’s revenue, leaving the foundry exposed to shifts in Nvidia-linked demand [19].
  • Nvidia’s $171.50 intraday high on Monday remained below its $173.73 close the day before Huang resigned [295].
  • Nvidia’s financing of AI startups that then buy its chips has drawn criticism as potentially circular; the company’s founder has argued it reflects genuine demand [63].

International Reactions

  • 🇨🇳 China: China’s state-run Global Times urged Beijing to remain vigilant after Huang’s return, signaling that his reinstatement did not mean Washington’s chip-export restrictions would ease [363].
  • 🇨🇳 China: China’s Foreign Ministry rejected the spying allegations as “entirely baseless vilification and smearing” after Huang’s affair with Alice Huang became public [70].
  • Alibaba: Alibaba described the U.S. move to place its Singaporean unit on the Entity List as “erroneous” and rejected allegations of chip diversion [39].

Q&A

Q: What investigations remain open beyond the board’s review of the intelligence concern?

A: Separate probes into Nvidia’s accounting and corporate governance are also reported [99].

Q: What evidence did the Nvidia board rely on to assess national-security concerns?

A: Nvidia said it received input from senior U.S. government officials but did not identify them or disclose what they told directors [183][255].

Q: What role will Colette Kress have now that Huang is back?

A: Kress, who stepped in as interim CEO, has returned to her role as Nvidia’s chief financial officer [16].
